México - List of Final Tables

Amateur Era (1902-1943)
Professional Era (since 1943)

Amateur Era

The Mexico Association Foot-ball League

The first soccer league in Mexico was created on July 19, 1902 and called: 
"The Mexico Association Foot-ball League".
Five teams took part, the first match was played on October 19, 1902. 

Tournament played at a single leg.

1902-03 
1. Orizaba AC                     4   3   1   0    4- 2   7 (champion)
----------------------------------------------------------- 
2. Reforma AC                     4   2   2   0   11- 5   6 [Mexico City] 
3. British Club                   4   2   0   2    7- 3   4 [Mexico City]
4. Pachuca AC                     4   0   2   2    3- 6   2 
5. México Cricket Club            4   0   1   3    1-10   1 [Mexico City]


1903-04  
1. México Cricket Club            8   7   0   1   13- 1  14 (champion) [Mexico City]
-----------------------------------------------------------
2. Reforma AC                     7   5   1   1   15- 3  11 [Mexico City] 
3. British Club                   5   1   1   3    1- 4   3 [Mexico City]
4. Orizaba AC                     6   1   1   4    2-11   3 
5. Pachuca AC                     6   0   1   5    2-14   1 

Changes for the next season:
Orizaba AC dissolved
Puebla AC joined the league
México Cricket Club dissolved
San Pedro Golf Club joined the league with most of players of México Cricket Club

Changes for the next season:
Club México comeback
Asturias joined the league


1919-20
The season started with nine teams (the same participants of 1918-19 season plus Asturias 
and Club México (comeback); however España and its filial, España de Veracruz withdrew 
after a terrible incidents in a friendly match against Atlas (Guadalajara).

Tournament played at a single leg.  
1. Pachuca AC                     5   3   2   0    7- 2   8 (champion)
-----------------------------------------------------------
2. Germania FV                    5   2   3   0    8- 4   7 [Mexico City]
3. Club México                    5   2   2   1    4- 3   6 [Mexico City]
4. Asturias                       5   2   1   2    5- 6   5 [Mexico City]
5. Centro Unión/América           5   1   1   3    8- 7   3 [Mexico City]
6. Deportivo Español              5   0   1   4    1-11   1 [Mexico City]
-----------------------------------------------------------
-. Tigres                         -   -   -   -     -     - (ejected)  [Mexico City]
-. España                         -   -   -   -     -     - (withdrew) [Mexico City]
-. España Veracruz                -   -   -   -     -     - (withdrew) [Mexico City]

NB: In mid season, Centro Unión changes back to its original name, América.
España FC changes its name to Real Club España.
Tigres ejected after did not show up for its last three matches (all its matches were annulled).

Changes for the next season:
Pachuca AC and Deportivo Español dissolved 
Deportivo Internacional and Morelos entered (replacing Pachuca and Deportivo Español)
RC España decided to form their own league called "Liga Nacional" playing alongside the official one (Liga Mexicana)
América withdrew from Liga Mexicana and joined Liga Nacional
L’Amicale Française and Reforma AC come back and joined Liga Nacional
Luz y Fuerza entered Liga Nacional

Changes for the next season:
 
Liga Mexicana: 
Deportivo Internacional and Morelos withdrew
Tranvías entered

Liga Nacional:
No changes 

In 1921 "Torneo del Centenario" was held to commemorate the 100 years of independence of Mexico. 
Teams from the two leagues (Mexicana and Nacional) plus provincial teams participated in this tournament. 
This one is not considered official as it is not organized by no official entity.
 

1921-22
(All clubs from Mexico City)

-----------------------------------------------------------

Liga Nacional (unofficial)

1. España              (playoff)                    
2. América             (playoff)               
----------------------
3. Luz y Fuerza
4. L’Amicale Française (withdrew)
5. Reforma             (withdrew)

NB: According to the few press reports available L'Amicale along with Reforma withdrew due to lack of players, 
    the league announced on Mar. 12, 1922 a single playoff match between España and América to define the championship.

Playoff 
Parque España (Reforma)
26/03/1922 España 2-0 América

Champion: España

Changes for the next season:

On August 28, 1922 emerged a new league called "Campeonato de Primera Fuerza de la Federación Mexicana de Football"
after the merge between the old Liga Mexicana and Liga Nacional.
L'Amicale Française dissolved, Reforma asked permission to not play next season and comeback for 1923-24.

Guerra y Marina entered


Campeonato de Primera Fuerza de la Federación Mexicana de Football

1930-31
(All club from Mexico City) 
Marte				  2   1   1   0	   7- 5	  3
RC España			  2   1   1   0	   6- 4	  3
Atlante				  2   1   1   0	   6- 5	  3
Germania FV			  2   1   1   0	   2- 1	  3
Necaxa				  2   0   2   0	   4- 4	  2
Asturias			  2   0   1   1	   3- 4	  1
Club México			  2   0   1   1	   2- 4	  1
América				  2   0   0   2	   6- 9	  0
*Standings at the moment of cancellation of the season

The tournament started, however RC España withdrew after the delayed return of Mexican 
National Team of the 1930 FIFA World Cup and for the illegal signing of player Gaspar Rubio of Real Madrid.
Then the tournament was cancelled and merged a new league called: Campeonato de la Liga 
Mayor del Distrito Federal.
RC España and Club México did not took part being replaced by Leones and Sporting.


Campeonato Preferente de la Liga Mayor del Distrito Federal

Championship Playoff
All the matches played in Parque Necaxa
21/08/1932 Atlante 3-2 Necaxa
28/08/1932 Atlante 1-1 Necaxa
04/09/1932 Atlante 1-0 Necaxa

Champion: Atlante

For the start of the next season the teams were divided in two groups, in the Group A (first level)
were seeded the four best teams of 1931-32 season (Atlante, Necaxa, Asturias and América) 
In the Group B (a kind of second level) were seeded the last four teams of 1931-32 season (Germania FV,
Sporting, Marte and Leones).

RC España and Club México comeback.


1932-33
(All clubs from México City)

At the end of the season the last team of Group A play a relegation playoff series against the
first team of Group B. 

Group A
1. Necaxa                         8   5   1   2   30-18  11 (champion) 
-----------------------------------------------------------

Professional Era

In 1943 the "Liga Mayor" (Major League) was formed. It was the first professional and true national league. Charter members were América (México), Asturias (México), Atlante (México), España (México), Marte (México) and Moctezuma (Orizaba) from the old "Primera Fuerza", Atlas (Guadalajara) and Guadalajara from the "Liga de Occidente" (Western League) and A.D.O (Orizaba) and Veracruz from the "Liga Veracruzana".

Changes for the next season:

Asturias, España and Moctezuma retired from the league due to differences with the Federation. 
Necaxa, an old "Primera Fuerza" member and who didn't accept professionalism in 1943, 
re-joined for next season.         

The Second Division started in 1950, thus the "Liga Mayor" changed to "Primera Division", 
every year one team is promoted and one is relegated.

This tournament was played before and after the 1970 World Cup. 
It is counted as a league championship.

Promoted: Zacatepec (Second Division Champion 1969-70 season) and Puebla (Winner of a
promotional tournament to increase the number of teams to 18 in which
Unión de Curtidores was 2nd., Nacional was 3rd. and Naucalpan was 4th.   

Originally, Laguna (last place of the 1969-70 season) had to play against the last place of the Consolation Group (Oro) in a 
playoff relegation series, however for expansion to 18 teams for the 1970-71 season this series was cancelled, 
so there was no relegation.

Changes for the next season:

Oro changed its name to Jalisco.

Season 1985-86

Due to the 1986 World Cup played in México the federation cancelled the 1985-86 league. 
Instead there were 2 short tournaments PRODE-85 and México-86. 
They are counted as league championships.

Changes for the next season:

The Irapuato Franchise that had played in Veracruz in the Verano 2002 moved to Tuxtla Gutierrez, Chiapas and took the
name Jaguares. The promoted Club Veracruz stayed in that city.

La Piedad moved to Queretaro and took the name Gallos Blancos.

Atlante moved to Ciudad Nezahualcoyotl.

Invierno Tournament will be called Apertura Tournament, Verano Tournament will be called Clausura Tournament.

Changes for the next season:

For 2004/05 the league is reduced to 18 clubs; Gallos Blancos and Irapuato were excluded, 
after a vote among the club owners.  
Queretaro will play at the 2nd level 2004/05 while Irapuato (who were also robbed of their franchise 
at the end of 2000/01 when their team was moved to Veracruz) will not play at all next season.

Dorados Culiacán changed it´s name to Dorados Sinaloa.
